m\geq C\rho_{\max}(d_{\mathrm{eff}}+\log(1/(\epsilon\delta))\log(d_{\mathrm{eff}}/\delta)/\epsilon^{2})
,
\rho_{\max}\equiv\max_{1\leq i\leq n}n\ell^{\mathbf{C}}_{i}(\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{A}/\sqrt{n})/d_{\mathrm{eff}}
and
\epsilon\in(0,1/2]
suffices to ensure that
\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm SRHT}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm SRHT}\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}
is an
(\epsilon,\delta)
-approximation of
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}
.
This leads to
\Pr(\zeta)\leq\delta/2
.
Combining the above results, we obtain
\Pr(\zeta\bigcap\zeta_{\omega})\leq\delta
.
This, together with

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}\check{\mathbf{Z}}_{3}\mathbf{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|=O\left(\frac{\log n}{\log(\log n)}\sqrt{\frac{\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{3}_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m^{3}}}\right)+O\left(\max\limits_{1\leq i\leq n}\left|\frac{\ell^{\mathbf{C}}_{i}(\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{A}/\sqrt{n})-d_{\mathrm{eff}}\pi_{i}}{m\pi_{i}}\right|\right),

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}\check{\mathbf{Z}}_{1}\mathbf{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|=O\left(\frac{1}{m}\right),~\|\mathbf{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}\check{\mathbf{Z}}_{2}\mathbf{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|=O\left(\sqrt{\frac{\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{3}_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m^{3}}}\right).

in
Section

E.3.2
, concludes the proof of
Corollary

3.7
.

\Box

Remark E.3

(SRHT inversion bias with different sampling sizes)
.

Recalling (
40
), we observe that

\rho_{\max}=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}n\ell^{\mathbf{C}}_{i}(\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{A}/\sqrt{n})/d_{\mathrm{eff}}\leq 2

holds with the probability at least
1-\delta/2
.
Consequently, choosing
m\geq C(d_{\mathrm{eff}}+\log(1/(\epsilon\delta))\log(d_{\mathrm{eff}}/\delta)/\epsilon^{2})
suffices to ensure that
\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm SRHT}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm SRHT}\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}
is an
(\epsilon,\delta)
-approximation (or subspace embedding) of
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}
.
On the other hand, note from
Corollary

3.7
that

1.

by taking
\nu=0
, a number of
m=\Theta(d_{\mathrm{eff}})
samples with
n\exp(-d_{\mathrm{eff}})<\delta<m^{-3}
, we have that
(\frac{m}{m-d_{\mathrm{eff}}}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm SRHT}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm SRHT}+\mathbf{C})^{-1}
is an
(O(1),\delta)
-unbiased estimator of
(\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{C})^{-1}
: This, in particular, agrees with the above subspace embedding condition up to logarithmic factor.

2.

taking
\nu>1
in
Corollary

3.7
allows one to (further) reduce the inversion bias (to a level that is significantly smaller than
O(1)
) by increasing the sample size
m=\Theta(d_{\mathrm{eff}}^{1+\nu})
.

Lemma E.4

(Row norms)
.

Let
\mathbf{H}_{n}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}
be the Walsh–Hadamard matrix of size
n\geq 4
as in
Definition

3.6
and
\mathbf{D}_{n}=\diag(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}
with
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
a Rademacher random vector.
Then, we have, for a matrix
\mathbf{X}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times d}
with
n\geq d
and
t\geq\max\{\|\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\|_{F}\sqrt{\log(2n/\delta)/(cn^{2})},\|\mathbf{X}\|^{2}\log(2n/\delta)/(cn)\}
that

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left|\frac{\|\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{X}\|^{2}}{n}-\frac{\|\mathbf{X}\|_{F}^{2}}{n}\right|\geq t,1\leq i\leq n\right)\leq\delta,

where
\|\cdot\|_{F}
denotes the Frobenius norm, and
c>0
is a universal constant.

Proof of
Lemma

E.4
.

Recall from
Definition

3.6
that both
\mathbf{H}_{n}
and
\mathbf{D}_{n}
are orthogonal matrices such that
\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{D}_{n}^{2}\mathbf{H}_{n}/n=\mathbf{I}_{n}
.
Fix a row index
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
and consider

\displaystyle\frac{\|\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{X}\|^{2}}{n}=\|\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\top}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{X}\|^{2},

where
\mathbf{E}=\diag(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{n}/\sqrt{n})
is a diagonal matrix formed from the
i^{th}
row of
\mathbf{H}_{n}/\sqrt{n}
.
Observe that
\mathbf{E}^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{I}_{n}
, we further have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\top}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{E}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}]=\tr({\mathbb{E}}[\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{E}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\top}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{X}])=\tr(\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{2}\mathbf{X})=\frac{\|\mathbf{X}\|^{2}_{F}}{n}.

Note that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{E}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{E}\|=\|\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{2}\mathbf{X}\|=\frac{\|\mathbf{X}\|^{2}}{n},

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{E}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{E}\|^{2}_{F}=\tr(\mathbf{E}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{2}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{E})=\tr(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{2}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{2})=\frac{\|\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\|^{2}_{F}}{n^{2}},

and

K=\max\limits_{1\leq i\leq n}\inf\{s>0:{\mathbb{E}}[\exp(\upsilon_{i}^{2}/s^{2})]\leq 2\}=(\log(2))^{-1/2}>1,

where
\upsilon_{i}
is the
i^{th}
variable of
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}
.
Apply Hanson-Wright inequality in
Theorem

A.5
with
t\geq\max\{\log(2)\|\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\|_{F}\sqrt{\log(2n/\delta)/(cn^{2})},\log(2)\|\mathbf{X}\|^{2}\log(2n/\delta)/(cn)\}
,
for each
i=1,\ldots,n
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left|\frac{\|\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{X}\|^{2}}{n}-\frac{\|\mathbf{X}\|_{F}^{2}}{n}\right|\geq t\right)\leq\frac{\delta}{n}.

Taking a union bound over these
n
events, we conclude the proof of
Lemma

E.4
.
∎

Appendix F
Proof of the Results in
Section

4

In this section, we present proof of the results in
Section

4
.
Precisely, to establish convergence guarantees for the bias-corrected SSN iteration in (
9
), we first introduce in
Proposition

F.1
a fine-grained non-asymptotic bound on second moment of the normalized sub-sampled inverse matrix in
Proposition

F.1
and present its proof in
Section

F.1
.
Stemming from this second inverse moment analysis, we also provide non-asymptotic bounds on the second inverse moment for the exact and/or approximate leverage sampling and SRHT in
Corollary

F.2
and
Corollary

F.3
, respectively, under scalar debiasing.
Then, in
Section

F.2
, we provide the detailed proof of
Theorem

4.3
.
Finally, further discussions and additional results related to
Theorem

4.3
are provided in
Section

F.3
.

Proposition F.1

(Fine-grained analysis of second inverse moment)
.

For a given matrix
\mathbf{A}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times d}
, let
\mathbf{S}
be a random sampling matrix with number of trials
m
and importance sampling distribution
\{\pi_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
as in
Definition

2.1
, and let
\mathbf{C}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
be a p.s.d. matrix and
\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{A}}=(\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{C})^{-1/2}\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{C})^{-1/2}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
.
Define
d_{\mathrm{eff}}=\tr(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}})
with
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}\equiv\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{C})^{-1/2}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times d}

and the fine-grained de-biased sampling matrix
\check{\mathbf{S}}=\diag\left\{\sqrt{m/(m-\ell^{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{s}}/\pi_{i_{s}})}\right\}_{s=1}^{m}\cdot\mathbf{S}
as in
Proposition

3.2
.
Then, for diagonal matrix
\bar{\mathbf{F}}=\diag\{\bar{F}_{ii}\}^{n}_{i=1}
with

\bar{F}_{ii}=\frac{{{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{i}}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{S}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}+\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{A}})^{-2}]{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{i}}{m\pi_{i}},

and
{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{i}^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
the
i^{th}
row of
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}
, there exists universal constant
C>0
independent of
n,d_{\mathrm{eff}}
such that for
m\geq C\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\log(d_{\mathrm{eff}}/\delta)+(\log n)^{2/3}/(\epsilon\log(\log n))^{2/3})
with
\delta\leq m^{-3}
and max factor
\rho_{\max}=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\ell^{\mathbf{C}}_{i}/(\pi_{i}d_{\mathrm{eff}})
in
Definition

2.3
,
(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{S}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}+\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{A}})^{-2}
is an
(\epsilon,\delta)
-unbiased estimator of
\mathbf{I}_{d}+{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}}
.

Note that
Proposition

F.1
can be seen as a second-order extension of (the first-order inversion bias in)
Proposition

3.2
.
As we shall see below in
Section

F.2
, this result is instrumental in establishing the convergence rate for SSN in
Theorem

4.3
.

F.1
Proof of
Proposition

F.1

Following the methodology of the proofs of
Theorem

3.1
and
Proposition

3.2
, the proof of
Proposition

F.1
also comes in the following two steps:

1.

construct an high probability event
\zeta
as in (
20
); and

2.

conditional on the event
\zeta
, derive a bound for the spectral norm
\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{S}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}+\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{A}})^{-2}]-(\mathbf{I}_{d}+\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}})\|
, using again “leave-one-out” type analysis.

First, let us recall some notations from the proofs of
Theorem

3.1
and
Proposition

3.2
.
For the ease of further use, denote

\check{\mathbf{S}}=\diag\left\{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}\right\}^{m}_{s=1}\cdot\mathbf{S},\quad i_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,n\},\quad F_{i_{s}i_{s}}=\sqrt{m/(m-\ell_{i_{s}}^{\mathbf{C}}/\pi_{i_{s}})},

\mathbf{H}=\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{C}
,
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}
, and
\hat{\mathbf{x}}^{\top}_{s}=\mathbf{e}^{\top}_{i_{s}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}/\sqrt{\pi_{i_{s}}}
such that
{\mathbb{E}}[\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}]=\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}
.
Further let

\hat{\mathbf{Q}}=(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{S}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}+\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{A}})^{-1}=\left(\sum^{m}_{s=1}\frac{1}{m}F_{i_{s}i_{s}}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}+\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)^{-1},~\text{and}~\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}=\left(\sum^{m}_{j\neq s}\frac{1}{m}F_{jj}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{j}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{j}^{\top}+\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)^{-1}.

To prove
Proposition

F.1
, we first rewrite

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}]-(\mathbf{I}_{d}+\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}})=\underbrace{{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}-\mathbf{I}_{d}]}_{\mathbf{T}_{1}}+{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}(\hat{\mathbf{Q}}-\mathbf{I}_{d})]-\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}.

(41)

Then, along with
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}+\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{A}}=\mathbf{I}_{d}
and let
\hat{\gamma}_{s}=1+\frac{1}{m}F_{i_{s}i_{s}}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}=\check{\gamma}_{s}
,
s=1,\ldots,m
, we get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}(\hat{\mathbf{Q}}-\mathbf{I}_{d})]={\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}(\hat{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}-\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{S}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}))]={\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}]-{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\sum^{m}_{s=1}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}}{m\hat{\gamma}_{s}}\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}]-{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\sum^{m}_{s=1}\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}}{m\check{\gamma}_{s}}\right]+{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\sum^{m}_{s=1}\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}^{2}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}}{m^{2}\check{\gamma}^{2}_{s}}\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}]-{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}}{\check{\gamma}_{s}}\right]+{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}^{2}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}}{m\check{\gamma}^{2}_{s}}\right]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}-\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\right]}_{\mathbf{T}_{2}}+\underbrace{{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\left(1-\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}}{\check{\gamma}_{s}}\right)\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\right]}_{\mathbf{T}_{3}}+{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}^{2}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}}{m\check{\gamma}^{2}_{s}}\right],

(42)

where the second and third equalities follow from the Sherman–Morrison formula.
Further define
{\bar{\mathbf{F}}}^{{}^{\prime}}=\diag\{{\bar{F}}^{{}^{\prime}}_{ii}\}^{n}_{i=1}
with
{\bar{F}}^{{}^{\prime}}_{ii}={{}_{\mathbf{C}}}^{\top}_{i}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}^{2}]{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{i}/m\pi_{i}
.
Using (
41
) and (
42
), we get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}]-(\mathbf{I}_{d}+\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{T}_{1}+\mathbf{T}_{2}+\mathbf{T}_{3}+{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}^{2}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}}{m\check{\gamma}^{2}_{s}}\right]-\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{T}_{1}+\mathbf{T}_{2}+\mathbf{T}_{3}+\underbrace{{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}-\mathbf{I}_{d}]\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{\mathbf{T}_{4}}+\underbrace{{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[(\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}-\hat{\mathbf{Q}})\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}]}_{\mathbf{T}_{5}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}^{{}^{\prime}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}-\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}})]}_{\mathbf{T}_{6}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\frac{\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}}{m}\right]-{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}^{{}^{\prime}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}})]}_{\mathbf{T}_{7}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\left(\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}^{2}}{\check{\gamma}^{2}_{s}}-1\right)\frac{\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}}{m}\right]}_{\mathbf{T}_{8}}.

At this point, note from
Proposition

3.2
that
\|\mathbf{T}_{1}\|=O\left(\sqrt{(\log n)^{2}\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{3}_{\mathrm{eff}}/((\log(\log n))^{2}m^{3})}\right)
. Using
\hat{\mathbf{Q}}=\mathbf{H}^{1/2}\check{\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{H}^{1/2}
, and noting the fact that
\zeta^{\prime}
implies that

\displaystyle\check{\mathbf{Q}}\preceq\check{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\preceq 6\mathbf{H}^{-1},

(43)

we have
\bar{F}_{ii}\leq{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}^{\top}_{i}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}]{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{i}/m\pi_{i}\leq 36\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}/m<1
,
which, together with
Proposition

3.2
yields that
\|\mathbf{T}_{4}\|=O\left(\sqrt{(\log n)^{2}\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{3}_{\mathrm{eff}}/((\log(\log n))^{2}m^{3})}\right)
.

Now, we proceed to bound
\mathbf{T}_{2}
. We first write

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{T}_{2}\|

\displaystyle=\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}]-{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}]\|+\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}]-{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}]\|

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[(\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}-\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s})\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}]\|}_{T_{21}}+\underbrace{\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}-\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top})]\|}_{T_{22}}.

Recalling
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}\preceq\mathbf{I}_{d}
, we get, for the first term
T_{21}
, that

\displaystyle T_{21}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}-\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]\|\leq\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}(\hat{\mathbf{Q}}-\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s})]\|+\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[(\hat{\mathbf{Q}}-\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s})\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}]\|

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}\left\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}}{m\check{\gamma}_{s}}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\right]\right\|+\left\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}}{m\check{\gamma}_{s}}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}2\left\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}}{m\check{\gamma}_{s}}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\right]\right\|+\left\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\frac{F^{2}_{i_{s}i_{s}}}{m^{2}\check{\gamma}^{2}_{s}}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\right]\right\|,

(44)

where we used (again) Sherman–Morrison formula twice in the step
(a)
and
(b)
, respectively.
For the first term in (
44
), using the fact that
F_{i_{s}i_{s}}/\check{\gamma}_{s}<F_{i_{s}i_{s}}<2
, together with (
23
) and (
43
), we get

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}}{m\check{\gamma}_{s}}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle=\sup\limits_{\|\mathbf{u}\|=1,\|\mathbf{v}\|=1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}}{m\check{\gamma}_{s}}\mathbf{u}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\mathbf{v}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{m}\sup\limits_{\|\mathbf{u}\|=1,\|\mathbf{v}\|=1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{{}^{\prime}}}\left[|\mathbf{u}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\mathbf{v}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{m}\sup\limits_{\|\mathbf{u}\|=1,\|\mathbf{v}\|=1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{{}^{\prime}}}\left[\mathbf{u}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{v}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\mathbf{v}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{m}\sup\limits_{\|\mathbf{u}\|=1,\|\mathbf{v}\|=1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{{}^{\prime}}}\left[\sum^{n}_{j=1}\mathbf{u}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}{}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}{}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{v}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}{}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}{}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\mathbf{v}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{m}\sup\limits_{\|\mathbf{u}\|=1,\|\mathbf{v}\|=1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{{}^{\prime}}}\left[\mathbf{u}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{v}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\mathbf{v}\right]

\displaystyle=O\Big(\frac{1}{m}\Big).

Analogously as above, we have, for the second term in (
44
) that

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\frac{F^{2}_{i_{s}i_{s}}}{m^{2}\check{\gamma}^{2}_{s}}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{m^{2}}\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{{}^{\prime}}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}]\|

\displaystyle=\frac{8}{m^{2}}\left\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{{}^{\prime}}}\left[\sum^{n}_{j=1}\frac{\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}^{\top}_{j}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}}{\pi_{j}}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle=\frac{288\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m^{2}}\left\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{{}^{\prime}}}\left[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\right]\right\|=O\Big(\frac{\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m^{2}}\Big).

We thus conclude that
T_{21}=O(1/m)
.

Following the methodology used to bound
\|\mathbf{H}^{1/2}\check{\mathbf{Z}}_{2}\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}\|
and
\|\mathbf{H}^{1/2}\check{\mathbf{Z}}_{3}\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}\|
in
Section

E.2
, together with (
43
), we get similarly

T_{22}=O\left(\sqrt{\frac{\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{3}_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m^{3}}}\right),\quad\|\mathbf{T}_{3}\|=O\left(\frac{\log n}{\log(\log n)}\sqrt{\frac{\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{3}_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m^{3}}}\right).

Utilizing the fact that
\bar{F}_{ii}\leq 36\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}/m
, together with (
36
) and (
43
), it follows that

\|\mathbf{T}_{5}\|\leq\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}-\hat{\mathbf{Q}}]\|\|\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}\|\leq O\left(\frac{\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m^{2}}\right).

Since (
43
) and
T_{21}=O(1/m)
, we can bound
\|\mathbf{T}_{6}\|
as

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{T}_{6}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}]\|\|\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}\|^{2}\|\bar{\mathbf{F}}^{{}^{\prime}}-\bar{\mathbf{F}}\|\leq 6\mathop{\max}\limits_{1\leq j\leq n}\frac{|{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}^{\top}_{j}({\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]-{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}]){{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}|}{m\pi_{j}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{6\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]-{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}]\|=O\Big(\frac{\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m^{2}}\Big).

We then move on to bound
\|\mathbf{T}_{7}\|
.
We start by rewriting

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{T}_{7}\|

\displaystyle=\left\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\left(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}^{{}^{\prime}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}-\frac{\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}}{m}\right)\right]\right\|\leq\underbrace{\left\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\left(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}^{{}^{\prime}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}-\frac{\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}}{m}\right)\right]\right\|}_{T_{71}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\left\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\frac{\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}({\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]-\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s})\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}}{m}\right]\right\|}_{T_{72}}+\underbrace{\left\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\frac{\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}({\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]-{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}])\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}}{m}\right]\right\|}_{T_{73}}.

Note that
{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{{}^{\prime}}}\Big[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\Big(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}^{{}^{\prime}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}-\frac{\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}}{m}\Big)\Big]=0
. By adapting the techniques of bounding
\|\mathbf{H}^{1/2}\check{\mathbf{Z}}_{2}\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}\|
in
Section

E.2
, for
\delta_{3}<m^{-3}
, we further get

\displaystyle T_{71}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}\left[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\left(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}^{{}^{\prime}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}-\frac{\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}}{m}\right)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\neg\zeta_{3}}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m^{4}}\right)+12{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}\left[\frac{\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}}{m}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\neg\zeta_{3}}\right].

Furthermore, applying the Chebyshev’s inequality again, and considering
{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}[\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}/m]\leq 36\rho_{\max}d^{2}_{\mathrm{eff}}/m
, alongside

\displaystyle\Var_{\zeta^{\prime}}\left[\frac{\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}}{m}\right]\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}\left[\frac{(\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s})^{2}}{m^{2}}\right]\leq\frac{6^{4}\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{4}_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m^{2}},

it follows that, for
x\geq 72\rho_{\max}d^{2}_{\mathrm{eff}}/m
,
\Pr(\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}/m\geq x~|~\zeta^{\prime})\leq 6^{4}\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{4}_{\mathrm{eff}}/(m^{2}x^{2})
.
Subsequently, by
\delta_{3}<m^{-3}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}\left[\frac{\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}}{m}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\neg\zeta_{3}}\right]

\displaystyle=\int^{\infty}_{0}\Pr\left(\frac{\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}}{m}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\neg\zeta_{3}}\geq x~\Big|~\zeta^{\prime}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq 2m^{2}\delta_{3}+\int^{\infty}_{2m^{2}}\Pr\left(\frac{\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}}{m}\geq x~\Big|~\zeta^{\prime}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{m}+\frac{6^{4}\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{4}_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m^{2}}\int^{\infty}_{2m^{2}}\frac{1}{x^{2}}dx=\frac{2}{m}+\frac{6^{4}\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{4}_{\mathrm{eff}}}{2m^{4}}=O\left(\sqrt{\frac{\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{3}_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m^{3}}}\right).

This leads to
T_{71}=O\left(\sqrt{\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{3}_{\mathrm{eff}}/m^{3}}\right)
.

Next, we show a bound on
T_{72}
.
Using again (
23
) and (
43
), we get

\displaystyle T_{72}

\displaystyle\leq\sup\limits_{\|\mathbf{u}\|=1,\|\mathbf{v}\|=1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\left|\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}({\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]-\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s})\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\right|\left|\frac{\mathbf{u}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{v}}{m}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup\limits_{\|\mathbf{u}\|=1,\|\mathbf{v}\|=1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{{}^{\prime}}}\left[\left|\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}({\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]-\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s})\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\right|\left|\frac{\mathbf{u}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{v}}{m}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sup\limits_{\|\mathbf{u}\|=1,\|\mathbf{v}\|=1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{{}^{\prime}}}\left[\left|\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}({\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]-\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s})\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\right|\left(\mathbf{u}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{v}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{v}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sup\limits_{\|\mathbf{u}\|=1,\|\mathbf{v}\|=1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{{}^{\prime}}}\left[\sum^{n}_{j=1}\frac{|{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}^{\top}({\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]-\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}){{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}|}{\pi_{j}}\left(\mathbf{u}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{v}^{\top}{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{v}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sup\limits_{\|\mathbf{u}\|=1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{{}^{\prime}}}\left[\max\limits_{1\leq j\leq n}\frac{|{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}^{\top}({\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]-\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}){{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}|}{\pi_{j}}\sum^{n}_{j=1}\mathbf{u}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\mathbf{u}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m}\sup\limits_{\|\mathbf{v}\|=1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{{}^{\prime}}}\left[\max\limits_{1\leq j\leq n}\frac{|{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}^{\top}({\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]-\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}){{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}|}{\pi_{j}}\sum^{n}_{j=1}\mathbf{v}^{\top}{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{v}\right]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{{}^{\prime}}}\left[\max\limits_{1\leq j\leq n}\frac{37|{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}^{\top}({\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]-\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}){{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}|}{m\pi_{j}}\right].

Observe that adapting the proof of
Lemma

D.1
, we can readily ascertain the following:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{{}^{\prime}}}\left[\max\limits_{1\leq j\leq n}\frac{37|{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}^{\top}({\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]-\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}){{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}|}{m\pi_{j}}\right]=O\left(\frac{\log n}{\log(\log n)}\sqrt{\frac{\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{3}_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m^{3}}}\right),

so that
T_{72}=O\left(\sqrt{(\log n)^{2}\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{3}_{\mathrm{eff}}/((\log(\log n))^{2}m^{3})}\right)
.
Recalling (
43
) again, we derive

\displaystyle T_{73}

\displaystyle\leq 12{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}\left[\left|\frac{\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}({\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]-{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}])\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}}{m}\right|\right]\leq 12\sum^{n}_{j=1}\left|\frac{{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}^{\top}({\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]-{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]){{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}^{\top}{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}}{m\pi_{j}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{12\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}\sum^{n}_{j=1}\left|{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}^{\top}({\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]-{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]){{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}\right|.

Along with

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]-{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]=\frac{\delta_{3}}{1-\delta_{3}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]-\frac{\delta_{3}}{1-\delta_{3}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}~|~\mathbf{1}_{\neg\zeta_{3}}],

we obtain, for
\delta_{3}\leq m^{-3}
,

\displaystyle|{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}^{\top}({\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]-{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]){{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}|\leq 2\delta_{3}{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}^{\top}{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}(\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}]\|+\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}~|~\mathbf{1}_{\neg\zeta_{3}}]\|)\leq\frac{144{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}^{\top}{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}}{m^{3}}.

This results in

T_{73}=O(\rho_{\max}d^{2}_{\mathrm{eff}}/m^{4})
.

Now, it remains to bound
\|\mathbf{T}_{8}\|
.
Noting (
43
) and
\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}}{\check{\gamma}_{s}}\leq 2
, it follows that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{T}_{8}\|

\displaystyle\leq\sup\limits_{\|\mathbf{u}\|=1,\|\mathbf{v}\|=1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\left|\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}^{2}}{\check{\gamma}^{2}_{s}}-1\right|\left|\frac{\mathbf{u}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{v}}{m}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup\limits_{\|\mathbf{u}\|=1,\|\mathbf{v}\|=1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\left|\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}}{\check{\gamma}_{s}}\left(\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}}{\check{\gamma}_{s}}-1\right)\right|\left|\frac{\mathbf{u}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{v}}{m}\right|\right]

\displaystyle+\sup\limits_{\|\mathbf{u}\|=1,\|\mathbf{v}\|=1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\left|\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}}{\check{\gamma}_{s}}-1\right|\left|\frac{\mathbf{u}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{v}}{m}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq 3\sup\limits_{\|\mathbf{u}\|=1,\|\mathbf{v}\|=1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\left|\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}}{\check{\gamma}_{s}}-1\right|\left|\frac{\mathbf{u}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{v}}{m}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{108\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}\sup\limits_{\|\mathbf{u}\|=1,\|\mathbf{v}\|=1}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\left|\frac{F_{i_{s}i_{s}}}{\check{\gamma}_{s}}-1\right|\left|\mathbf{u}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{-s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{v}\right|\right].

Further, applying the techniques of bounding

\|\mathbf{H}^{1/2}\check{\mathbf{Z}}_{3}\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}\|
in
Section

E.2
again, we get
\|\mathbf{T}_{8}\|=O\left(\sqrt{(\log n)^{2}\rho_{\max}^{5}d^{5}_{\mathrm{eff}}/((\log(\log n))^{2}m^{5})}\right)
.
Putting the above together, we conclude that

\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}]-(\mathbf{I}_{d}+\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}})\|=O\left(\frac{\log n}{\log(\log n)}\sqrt{\frac{\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{3}_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m^{3}}}\right).

This thus concludes the proof of
Proposition

F.1
.

\Box

Based on the second inverse moment result in
Proposition

F.1
, we follow the line of arguments in
Corollary

3.4
and
Corollary

3.7
,to derive the following fine-grained second inverse moment results for exact and/or approximate leverage score sampling and SRHT using scalar debiasing.
The proofs of these results follow from a direct combination of the proofs of
Corollary

3.4
,
Corollary

3.7
and
Proposition

F.1
, and are omitted here.

Corollary F.2

(Second inverse moment using scalar debiasing under approximate leverage)
.

Under the settings and notations of
Proposition

F.1
, for any random sampling scheme with sampling distribution
\pi_{i}\in[\ell^{\mathbf{C}}_{i}/(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\rho_{\max}),\ell^{\mathbf{C}}_{i}/(d_{\mathrm{eff}}\rho_{\min})]
with some
\rho_{\min}\in[1/2,1]
as in
Definition

2.3
and diagonal matrix
\bar{\mathbf{F}}=\diag\{\bar{F}_{ii}\}^{n}_{i=1}
with

\bar{F}_{ii}=\frac{{{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{i}}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[(\frac{m}{m-d_{\mathrm{eff}}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{\top}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}+\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{A}})^{-2}]{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{i}}{m\pi_{i}}.

Then, there exists universal constant
C>0
independent of
n,d_{\mathrm{eff}}
such that for
m\geq C\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}\log(d_{\mathrm{eff}}/\delta)
with
\delta\leq m^{-3}
,
(\frac{m}{m-d_{\mathrm{eff}}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{\top}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}+\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{A}})^{-2}
is an
(\epsilon,\delta)
-unbiased estimator of
\mathbf{I}_{d}+{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}}
with inversion bias
\epsilon=\max\left\{\tilde{O}\left(\sqrt{\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{3}_{\mathrm{eff}}/m^{3}}\right),O(\epsilon_{\rho}\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}/m)\right\}
for
\epsilon_{\rho}=\max\{\rho_{\min}^{-1}-1,1-\rho_{\max}^{-1}\}
.

Corollary F.3

(Second moment for SRHT using scalar debiasing )
.

Under the settings and notations of
Proposition

F.1
,
for
\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm SRHT}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m\times n}
the SRHT of
\mathbf{A}
as in
Definition

3.6
, and diagonal matrix
\bar{\mathbf{F}}=\diag\{\bar{F}_{ii}\}^{n}_{i=1}
with

\bar{F}_{ii}=\frac{\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}[(\frac{m}{m-d_{\mathrm{eff}}}\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm SRHT}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm SRHT}\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}+\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{A}})^{-2}]\mathbf{A}^{\top}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{e}_{i}}{nm\pi_{i}},

then there exists universal constant
C>0
independent of
n,d_{\mathrm{eff}}
and
n\exp(-d_{\mathrm{eff}})<\delta<m^{-3}
such that for
m\geq C\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}\log(d_{\mathrm{eff}}/\delta)
with max factor
\rho_{\max}=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}n\ell^{\mathbf{C}}_{i}(\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{A}/\sqrt{n})/d_{\mathrm{eff}}
,
(\frac{m}{m-d_{\mathrm{eff}}}\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm SRHT}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm SRHT}\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}+\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{A}})^{-2}
is an
(\epsilon,\delta)
-unbiased estimator of
\mathbf{I}_{d}+\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{A}^{\top}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}\bar{\mathbf{F}}\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}
with inversion bias
\epsilon=\tilde{O}\left(\sqrt{\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{3}_{\mathrm{eff}}/m^{3}}\right)+O\left(\sqrt{d_{\mathrm{eff}}\log(n/\delta)}/m\right)
.

These results are extension of the results in Corollaries in
3.4
and
3.7
, and can be used to derive SSN local convergence rates under exact/approximate leverage and SRHT similar to
Theorem

4.3
.
See Corollaries
F.5
and
F.6
in
Section

F.3
.

F.2
Proof of
Theorem

4.3

Here, we prove
Theorem

4.3
by adapting the proof approaches of our Propositions
3.2
and
F.1
and
Dereziński et al. [2021a, Theorem 10]
.
The proof of
Theorem

4.3
comes in the following two steps:

•

we first establish
Lemma

F.4
that connects the de-biased SSN iterations
\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}
to the true
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}
; and

•

perform detailed convergence analysis of de-biased SSN.

To begin, we define a high probability event
\zeta_{t}
for each
t=0,\ldots,T-1
, as defined in (
20
).
These events are established independently for each iteration, and we denote
\zeta=\bigcap^{T-1}_{t=0}\zeta_{t}
.
Under the setting of
Theorem

4.3
, it follows from
Lemma

2.7
that
\Pr(\zeta_{t})\geq 1-\delta/T
and
\Pr(\zeta)\geq 1-\delta
.
These events will be constantly exploited in the remainder of the proof of
Theorem

4.3
.

Proceeding to the core of our proof, we present an auxiliary lemma, which uses Propositions
3.2
and
F.1
to connect the de-biased iteration
\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}
to the true Newton iteration
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}
.
This is pivotal to prove
Theorem

4.3
.
Note that this result is universally applicable to any de-biased
\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}
and is independent of the smoothness assumption on
F
in, e.g.,
Assumption

4.2
.

Lemma F.4
.

Let
\mathbf{H}_{t}=\nabla^{2}f(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})+\mathbf{C}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
with
\nabla^{2}f(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})=\mathbf{A}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})^{\top}\mathbf{A}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
and
\mathbf{C}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})=\nabla^{2}\Phi(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
a p.s.d. matrix, and let
\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}
be the de-biased SSN iteration as in (
10
) with de-biased
\check{\mathbf{S}}_{t}
as in
Proposition

3.2
.
Denote
\Delta_{t}=\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast}
and
\check{\Delta}_{t}=\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast}
. If the exact Newton step
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}=\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-\mu_{t}\mathbf{H}^{-1}_{t}\mathbf{g}_{t}
(with
\mathbf{g}_{t}
the gradient of
F
at
\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}
) is a descent direction, i.e.,
\|\Delta_{t+1}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\leq\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}
, then, there exists universal constant
C>0
independent of
n,d_{\mathrm{eff}}
such that for
m\geq C\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\log(d_{\mathrm{eff}}T/\delta)+(\log n)^{2/3}/(\epsilon\log(\log n))^{2/3})
with
\epsilon>0
,
\delta\leq m^{-3}
,
we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta_{t}}[\|\check{\Delta}_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}]\leq\|\Delta_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}+\epsilon\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}+\frac{36\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}-\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}\|^{2}_{\nabla^{2}f(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})}.

Here,
\rho_{\max}
is the max importance sampling approximation factor in
Definition

2.3
for
\ell_{i}^{\mathbf{C}}=\max_{1\leq t\leq T}\ell_{i}^{\mathbf{C}}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
and
d_{\mathrm{eff}}=\max_{1\leq t\leq T}d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
with
\ell_{i}^{\mathbf{C}}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
and
d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
the leverage scores and effective dimension of
\mathbf{A}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
given
\mathbf{C}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
, respectively.

Proof of
Lemma

F.4
.

In both this proof and the subsequent proof of
Theorem

4.3
, to simplify the notation, we abbreviate

\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
as
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}
.
We first recall some notations from (the proof of)
Proposition

F.1
.
Let
\mathbf{p}_{t}=\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}-\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}=-\mu_{t}\mathbf{H}_{t}^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{t}
, and
\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{t}=(\mathbf{A}^{\top}_{\mathbf{C}}\check{\mathbf{S}}^{\top}_{t}\check{\mathbf{S}}_{t}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}+\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{A}})^{-1}
with the de-biased sampling matrix
\check{\mathbf{S}}_{t}=\diag\left\{\sqrt{m/(m-\ell^{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{s}}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})/\pi_{i_{s}})}\right\}_{s=1}^{m}\cdot\mathbf{S}_{t}
as in
Proposition

3.2
and
\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{A}}=\mathbf{H}_{t}^{-1/2}\mathbf{C}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})\mathbf{H}_{t}^{-1/2}
.
Define the diagonal matrix
\bar{\mathbf{F}}=\diag\{\bar{F}_{ii}\}^{n}_{i=1}
with
\bar{F}_{ii}={{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{i}}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta_{t}}[(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{S}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}+\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{A}})^{-2}]{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{i}/m\pi_{i}
as in
Proposition

F.1
and
{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{i}^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
the
i^{th}
row of
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}
.

Building on the results from
Proposition

3.2
and
Proposition

F.1
, for
\epsilon=O\left(\frac{\log n}{\log(\log n)}\sqrt{\frac{\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{3}_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m^{3}}}\right)
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta_{t}}[\|\check{\Delta}_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}]-\|\Delta_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}={\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta_{t}}[\|\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}]+2\Delta_{t+1}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta_{t}}[\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}]

\displaystyle=2\mu_{t}\Delta_{t+1}^{\top}\mathbf{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta_{t}}[(\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{t}-\mathbf{I}_{d})]\mathbf{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{t}\mathbf{H}_{t}^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{t}+\mu^{2}_{t}\mathbf{g}^{\top}_{t}\mathbf{H}^{-1}_{t}\mathbf{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta_{t}}[(\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{t}-\mathbf{I}_{d})^{2}]\mathbf{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{t}\mathbf{H}_{t}^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\Delta_{t+1}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\|\mathbf{p}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta_{t}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{t}]-\mathbf{I}_{d}\|+\mathbf{p}^{\top}_{t}\mathbf{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta_{t}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{t}^{2}-\mathbf{I}_{d}-2(\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{t}-\mathbf{I}_{d})]\mathbf{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{t}\mathbf{p}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\Delta_{t+1}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\|\mathbf{p}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta_{t}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{t}]-\mathbf{I}_{d}\|+2\|\mathbf{p}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta_{t}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{t}]-\mathbf{I}_{d}\|+\mathbf{p}^{\top}_{t}\mathbf{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta_{t}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{t}^{2}-\mathbf{I}_{d}-\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}_{t}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}]\mathbf{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{t}\mathbf{p}_{t}

\displaystyle+\mathbf{p}^{\top}_{t}\mathbf{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{t}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}_{t}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{t}\mathbf{p}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq 2\epsilon\|\Delta_{t+1}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\|\mathbf{p}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}+2\epsilon\|\mathbf{p}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}+\|\mathbf{p}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta_{t}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{t}^{2}]-\mathbf{I}_{d}-\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}_{t}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}\|+\|\mathbf{p}_{t}\|^{2}_{\nabla^{2}f(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})}\|\bar{\mathbf{F}}_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon\|\Delta_{t+1}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\|\mathbf{p}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}+\epsilon\|\mathbf{p}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}+\|\mathbf{p}_{t}\|^{2}_{\nabla^{2}f(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})}\max\limits_{1\leq i\leq n}\frac{{{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{i}}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta_{t}}[\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{t}^{2}]{{}_{\mathbf{C}}}_{i}}{m\pi_{i}}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\|\mathbf{p}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}+\epsilon\|\mathbf{p}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}+\frac{36\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}\|\mathbf{p}_{t}\|^{2}_{\nabla^{2}f(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})},

which combined with
\|\mathbf{p}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\leq\|\Delta_{t+1}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}+\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\leq 2\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}
and
\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\|\mathbf{p}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\leq 2\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}
leads to

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta_{t}}[\|\check{\Delta}_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}]-\|\Delta_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\epsilon\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}+4\epsilon\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}+\frac{36\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}\|\mathbf{p}_{t}\|^{2}_{\nabla^{2}f(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})}

\displaystyle=\epsilon\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}+\frac{36\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}\|\mathbf{p}_{t}\|^{2}_{\nabla^{2}f(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})}.

This concludes the proof of
Lemma

F.4
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

4.3
.

First note that by
Assumption

4.2
, we have the following result

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{H}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}-\mathbf{g}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}^{-1}}\leq\upsilon\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}},~~~~~\mathbf{H}_{t}\approx_{\upsilon}\mathbf{H},~\text{for}~\upsilon=O\left(\frac{\log n}{\log(\log n)}\sqrt{\frac{\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{3}_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m^{3}}}\right).

(45)

Letting
\check{\Delta}_{t}=\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast}
and
\Delta_{t}=\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast}
, we then show (
45
) holds under
Assumption

4.2
.
Consider
\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}\in U
for all
t
,
we get the following condition:

\displaystyle\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}}<\frac{\log n}{\log(\log n)}\left(\frac{\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}\sigma_{\min}}{m}\right)^{3/2}/L,

where
\sigma_{\min}
denotes the smallest eigenvalue of
\mathbf{H}
.
We further derive

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{H}_{t}-\mathbf{H})\mathbf{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}}\|\mathbf{H}_{t}-\mathbf{H}\|\leq\frac{L}{\sigma_{\min}}\|\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast}\|=\frac{L}{\sigma_{\min}}\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|\leq\frac{L}{\sigma^{3/2}_{\min}}\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\log n}{\log(\log n)}\sqrt{\frac{\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{3}_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m^{3}}}=\upsilon,

which leads to that
\mathbf{H}_{t}\approx_{\upsilon}\mathbf{H}
.
We now advance to verify the second part of (
45
).
Recalling that the standard analysis of Newton’s method
[
Boyd and Vandenberghe, 2004
]
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{H}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}-\mathbf{g}_{t}\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\mathbf{H}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}-\left(\int^{1}_{0}\nabla^{2}F(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast}+\tau\check{\Delta}_{t})d\tau\right)\check{\Delta}_{t}\right\|\leq\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|\int^{1}_{0}\|\nabla^{2}F(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})-\nabla^{2}F(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast}+\tau\check{\Delta}_{t})\|d\tau

\displaystyle\leq\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|\int^{1}_{0}(1-\tau)L\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|d\tau=\frac{L}{2}\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2},

which together with
\upsilon<1/2
and the fact that
\mathbf{H}_{t}\approx_{\upsilon}\mathbf{H}
implies that
\|\mathbf{H}_{t}^{-1}\|\leq\frac{1+\upsilon}{\sigma_{\min}}
achieves

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{H}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}-\mathbf{g}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1+\upsilon}{\sigma_{\min}}}\|\mathbf{H}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}-\mathbf{g}_{t}\|\leq\sqrt{\frac{L^{2}(1+\upsilon)}{4\sigma_{\min}}}\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}\leq\sqrt{\frac{L^{2}(1+\upsilon)}{4\sigma^{3}_{\min}}}\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}}\leq\frac{\upsilon\sqrt{1+\upsilon}}{2}\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\upsilon(1+\upsilon)^{3/2}}{2}\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\leq\upsilon\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}.

Thus, (
45
) holds.

Next, we proceed to the main part of the proof. We first rewrite
\|\Delta_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}
as

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{t+1}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}-\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}+\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast})=\Delta_{t+1}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}-\mu_{t}\Delta_{t+1}^{\top}\mathbf{g}_{t}

\displaystyle=(1-\mu_{t})\Delta_{t+1}^{\top}\mathbf{g}_{t}+\Delta_{t+1}^{\top}(\mathbf{H}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}-\mathbf{g}_{t})

\displaystyle=(1-\mu_{t})\Delta_{t+1}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}-(1-\mu_{t})\Delta_{t+1}^{\top}(\mathbf{H}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}-\mathbf{g}_{t})+\Delta_{t+1}^{\top}(\mathbf{H}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}-\mathbf{g}_{t})

\displaystyle=(1-\mu_{t})(\check{\Delta}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}-\mu_{t}\mathbf{g}_{t}^{\top}\check{\Delta}_{t})+\mu_{t}\Delta_{t+1}^{\top}(\mathbf{H}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}-\mathbf{g}_{t})

\displaystyle=(1-\mu_{t})^{2}\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}+\mu_{t}(\Delta_{t+1}+(1-\mu_{t})\check{\Delta}_{t})^{\top}(\mathbf{H}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}-\mathbf{g}_{t}).

Invoking (
45
) and triangle inequality, we get

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\leq(1-\mu_{t})^{2}\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}+\upsilon\mu_{t}\|\Delta_{t+1}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}+\upsilon\mu_{t}(1-\mu_{t})\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}.

With the fact that if
x^{2}\leq ax+b
then
x^{2}\leq a^{2}+2b
, it follows that:

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\upsilon^{2}\mu^{2}_{t}\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}+2(1-\mu_{t})^{2}\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}+2\upsilon\mu_{t}(1-\mu_{t})\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}

\displaystyle=(2(1-\mu_{t})^{2}+2\upsilon\mu_{t}+\mu^{2}_{t}(\upsilon^{2}-2\upsilon))\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq 2((1-\mu_{t})^{2}+\upsilon\mu_{t})\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}.

It then follows from
Lemma

F.4
and the inequality
\nabla^{2}f(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})\preceq\mathbf{H}_{t}
that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta_{t}}[\|\check{\Delta}_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}]-\|\Delta_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\upsilon\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}+\frac{36\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}\|\mathbf{p}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}.

(46)

For the second term in (
46
), we rewrite

\displaystyle\frac{36\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}\|\mathbf{p}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}

\displaystyle=\frac{36\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}\mu^{2}_{t}(\mathbf{g}_{t}^{\top}\check{\Delta}_{t}-\mathbf{g}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{t}^{-1}(\mathbf{H}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}-\mathbf{g}_{t}))

\displaystyle=\frac{36\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}\mu^{2}_{t}(\check{\Delta}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}-\check{\Delta}_{t}^{\top}(\mathbf{H}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}-\mathbf{g}_{t})-\mathbf{g}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{t}^{-1}(\mathbf{H}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}-\mathbf{g}_{t}))

\displaystyle=\frac{36\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}\mu^{2}_{t}\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}-\frac{36\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}\mu^{2}_{t}(\check{\Delta}_{t}+\mathbf{H}_{t}^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{t})^{\top}(\mathbf{H}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}-\mathbf{g}_{t})).

Using again (
45
), we further get

\displaystyle-\frac{36\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}\mu^{2}_{t}(\check{\Delta}_{t}+\mathbf{H}_{t}^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{t})^{\top}(\mathbf{H}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}-\mathbf{g}_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{36\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}(-\mu^{2}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}+\mu_{t}(\Delta_{t+1}-\check{\Delta}_{t}))^{\top}(\mathbf{H}_{t}\check{\Delta}_{t}-\mathbf{g}_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{36\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}\upsilon}{m}\mu_{t}(\mu_{t}+2)\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}.

Then, putting the above together, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta_{t}}[\|\check{\Delta}_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}]

\displaystyle\leq\left((2(1-\mu_{t})^{2}+2\upsilon\mu_{t}+\upsilon+\frac{36\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}\mu^{2}_{t}+\frac{36\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}\upsilon}{m}\mu_{t}(\mu_{t}+2)\right)\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}

\displaystyle=\left(\left(2(1-\mu_{t})^{2}+\frac{36\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}\mu^{2}_{t}\right)+\upsilon\left(2\mu_{t}+1+\frac{36\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}\mu_{t}(\mu_{t}+2)\right)\right)\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}},

which along with
\upsilon=O\left(\sqrt{(\log n)^{2}\rho_{\max}^{3}d^{3}_{\mathrm{eff}}/((\log(\log n))^{2}m^{3})}\right)
and
\mu_{t}=\frac{1}{1+\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}/m}<1
results in

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta_{t}}[\|\check{\Delta}_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\left(2(1-\mu_{t})^{2}+\frac{36\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}\mu^{2}_{t}\right)+\upsilon\right)\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\leq\left(\frac{37\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}+\upsilon\right)\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}.

Applying
\upsilon<\frac{1}{2}
and the fact that
\mathbf{H}_{t}\approx_{\upsilon}\mathbf{H}
indicates
\|\mathbf{v}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\approx_{\upsilon}\|\mathbf{v}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}}
, we get the following bound:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta_{t}}[\|\check{\Delta}_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}}]

\displaystyle\leq(1+\upsilon){\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta_{t}}[\|\check{\Delta}_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}]\leq(1+\upsilon)\left(\frac{37\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}+\upsilon\right)\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\upsilon)^{2}\left(\frac{37\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}+\upsilon\right)\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}}=\left(\frac{\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}+\upsilon\right)\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}},

(47)

where the constant “
(1+\upsilon)^{2}\cdot 37
” is absorbed into
m
.

Now, it remains to check that
\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}\in U
for all
t
when conditioned on the event
\zeta=\bigcap^{T-1}_{t=0}\zeta_{t}
. Assuming that this holds for
t=0
, then it remains to prove that, conditioned on the event
\zeta
,
\|\check{\Delta}_{t+1}\|_{\mathbf{H}}\leq\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}}
holds for each
t
almost surely. Following the fact that
Proposition

3.2
implies that, conditioned on
\zeta_{t}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{t}-\mathbf{I}_{d}\|\leq\varepsilon,

where Lemma
2.7
guarantees that
\varepsilon
is small. Adapting the techniques used in the proof of Lemma
F.4
and by the analysis of the exact Newton step
\Delta_{t+1}
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\|\check{\Delta}_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Delta_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}+2\|\Delta_{t+1}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\|\mathbf{p}_{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\|\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{t}-\mathbf{I}_{d}\|+\mathbf{p}^{\top}_{t}\mathbf{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{t}(\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{t}-\mathbf{I}_{d})^{2}\mathbf{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{t}\mathbf{p}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Delta_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}+8\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\|\hat{\mathbf{Q}}_{t}-\mathbf{I}_{d}\|\leq\left(\varepsilon+\frac{\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}+\upsilon\right)\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}.

From Lemma
2.7
, it is deduced that
\varepsilon+\frac{\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}+\upsilon
is sufficiently small. Using
\mathbf{H}_{t}\approx_{\upsilon}\mathbf{H}
again, we deduce
\|\check{\Delta}_{t+1}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}}\leq\|\check{\Delta}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}}
. Consequently, we infer that every iterate lies within
U
, and the result (
47
) holds for
t=0,1,\ldots,T-1
.
Putting the above together, we conclude that, conditioned on the event
\zeta
that holds with probability at least
1-\delta
, the union bound (
11
) is achieved, thereby completing the proof.

\Box

F.3
Additional Results on SSN and Further Discussions

Based on the above analysis, for any sampling method whose sampling probabilities are close to the exact approximate leverage scores, we then show that using scalar debiasing
m/(m-d_{\mathrm{eff}})
yields a slightly weaker local convergence result for SSN than that in
Theorem

4.3
, yet achieves enhanced computational efficiency. The result naturally follows by
recalling
Corollary

3.4
and
Corollary

F.2
, and adapting the proof of
Theorem

4.3
with a neighborhood
U=\{\boldsymbol{\beta}\colon\|\boldsymbol{\beta}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast}\|_{\mathbf{H}}<u_{\rm lev}\sigma_{\min}^{3/2}/L\}
in place of
U=\{\boldsymbol{\beta}\colon\|\boldsymbol{\beta}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast}\|_{\mathbf{H}}<(\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}\sigma_{\min}/m)^{3/2}/L\}
, and the corresponding proof is omitted for brevity.

Corollary F.5

(Local convergence of SSN using scalar debiasing under approximate leverage )
.

Under the settings and notations of
Theorem

4.3
,
for any random sampling scheme with sampling distribution

\pi_{i}\in[\ell^{\mathbf{C}}_{i}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})/(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})\rho_{\max}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})),\ell^{\mathbf{C}}_{i}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})/(d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})\rho_{\min}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}))]
as in
Corollary

3.4
, there exists a neighborhood
U
of
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast}
such that the
de-biased
SSN iteration
\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}=\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-\mu_{t}\left(\frac{m}{m-d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})}\mathbf{A}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{t}\mathbf{A}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})+\mathbf{C}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})\right)^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{t}
starting from
\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\in U
satisfies, for
U=\{\boldsymbol{\beta}\colon\|\boldsymbol{\beta}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast}\|_{\mathbf{H}}<u_{\rm lev}\sigma_{\min}^{3/2}/L\}
, step size
\mu_{t}=1-\frac{\rho_{\max}}{m/d_{\mathrm{eff}}+\rho_{\max}}
, and
m\geq C\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}\log(d_{\mathrm{eff}}T/\delta)
, that

\left({\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\frac{\|\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{T}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast}\|_{\mathbf{H}}}{\|\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast}\|_{\mathbf{H}}}\right]\right)^{1/T}\leq\frac{\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}(1+\epsilon+\epsilon_{\rho}),

holds for
\epsilon=\tilde{O}\left(\sqrt{\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}/m}\right),\epsilon_{\rho}=\max\{\rho_{\min}^{-1}-1,1-\rho_{\max}^{-1}\}
and conditioned on an event
\zeta
that happens with probability at least
1-\delta
. Here,
u_{\rm lev}=\log n(\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}/m)^{3/2}/\log(\log n)+\epsilon_{\rho}\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}/m
,
\sigma_{\min}
is the smallest singular value of
\mathbf{H}\equiv\mathbf{A}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast})^{\top}\mathbf{A}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast})+\mathbf{C}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast})
,
d_{\mathrm{eff}}=\max_{1\leq t\leq T}d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
with
\ell_{i}^{\mathbf{C}}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
and
d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
the leverage scores and effective dimension of
\mathbf{A}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
given
\mathbf{C}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
, respectively,
\rho_{\max}=\max_{1\leq t\leq T}\rho_{\max}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
and
\rho_{\min}=\min_{1\leq t\leq T}\rho_{\min}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
with
\rho_{\max}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
and
\rho_{\min}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
the (max and min) importance sampling approximation factors (of
\mathbf{A}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
given
\mathbf{C}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
) in
Definition

2.3
.

Similarly, for SRHT, applying the scalar debiasing
m/(m-d_{\mathrm{eff}})
to SSN also leads to a slightly weaker local convergence than that in
Theorem

4.3
but offers greater computational efficiency. This follows readily by
recalling
Corollary

3.7
and
Corollary

F.3
, and adapting the proof of
Theorem

4.3
with a neighborhood
U=\{\boldsymbol{\beta}\colon\|\boldsymbol{\beta}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast}\|_{\mathbf{H}}<u_{\rm SRHT}\sigma_{\min}^{3/2}/L\}
instead of
U=\{\boldsymbol{\beta}\colon\|\boldsymbol{\beta}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast}\|_{\mathbf{H}}<(\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}\sigma_{\min}/m)^{3/2}/L\}
, and we omit the detailed proof for brevity.

Corollary F.6

(Local convergence of SSN for SRHT using scalar debiasing)
.

Under the settings and notations of
Theorem

4.3
,
for
\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm SRHT}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})=\mathbf{S}_{t}\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{A}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})/\sqrt{n}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m\times n}
the SRHT of
\mathbf{A}
as in
Definition

3.6
, there exists a neighborhood
U
of
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast}
such that the
de-biased
SSN iteration
\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}=\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-\mu_{t}\left(\frac{m}{m-d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm SRHT}^{\top}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})\mathbf{S}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm SRHT}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})+\mathbf{C}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})\right)^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{t}
starting from
\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\in U
satisfies, for
U=\{\boldsymbol{\beta}\colon\|\boldsymbol{\beta}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast}\|_{\mathbf{H}}<u_{\rm SRHT}\sigma_{\min}^{3/2}/L\}
, step size
\mu_{t}=1-\frac{\rho_{\max}}{m/d_{\mathrm{eff}}+\rho_{\max}}
,
m\geq C\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}\log(d_{\mathrm{eff}}T/\delta)
, and
Tn\exp(-d_{\mathrm{eff}})<\delta<Tm^{-3}
, that

\left({\mathbb{E}}_{\zeta}\left[\frac{\|\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{T}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast}\|_{\mathbf{H}}}{\|\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast}\|_{\mathbf{H}}}\right]\right)^{1/T}\leq\frac{\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}}{m}(1+\epsilon),

(48)

holds for
\epsilon=\tilde{O}\left(\sqrt{\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}/m}\right)+O\left(\rho^{-1}_{\max}\sqrt{d_{\mathrm{eff}}^{-1}\log(nT/\delta)}\right)
and conditioned on an event
\zeta
that happens with probability at least
1-\delta
. Here,
u_{\rm SRHT}=\log n(\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}/m)^{3/2}/\log(\log n)+\sqrt{d_{\mathrm{eff}}\log(nT/\delta)}/m
,
\sigma_{\min}
is the smallest singular value of
\mathbf{H}\equiv\mathbf{A}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast})^{\top}\mathbf{A}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast})+\mathbf{C}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\ast})
,
d_{\mathrm{eff}}=\max_{1\leq t\leq T}d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
with
\ell_{i}^{\mathbf{C}}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
and
d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
the leverage scores and effective dimension of
\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{A}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})/\sqrt{n}
given
\mathbf{C}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
, respectively,
\rho_{\max}=\max_{1\leq t\leq T}\rho_{\max}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
with
\rho_{\max}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
the max importance sampling approximation factor (of
\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{A}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})/\sqrt{n}
given
\mathbf{C}(\check{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t})
) in
Definition

2.3
.

Remark F.7

(Comparison between
Corollary

F.6
and
Lacotte et al. [2021, Theorem 2]
)
.

Using
Corollary

3.7
and
Corollary

F.3
with
\nu=0
, it follows, by adapting the proof of
Theorem

4.3
, that for a number
m>Cd_{\mathrm{eff}}
of samples, the linear convergence rate in
Corollary

F.6
holds
but
with a right-hand side term of
O(1)
in (
48
).
That is similar to the
problem-dependent
linear convergence rate obtained in e.g.,
Lacotte et al. [2021, Theorem 2]
for self-concordant
f
,
\Phi
, and
F
in (
7
).
Notably, using a larger number of samples
m
as in
Corollary

F.6
(than
\Theta(d_{\mathrm{eff}})
), the (linear) convergence rate in
Corollary

F.6
becomes dependent on
\rho_{\max}d_{\mathrm{eff}}/m
, whereas the linear or quadratic rates stated in
Lacotte et al. [2021, Theorem 2]
cannot be characterized using
n
,
d_{\mathrm{eff}}
or
m
.

Appendix G
Additional Numerical Experiments and Implementation Details

Here, we provide in
Section

G.1
and
Section

G.2
implementation details of the numerical experiments on SSN in
Section

5
, and then in
Section

G.3
additional numerical results on the inversion bias under approximate versus exact leverage score sampling.

G.1
Sketching Matrices and Step Size

For a data matrix
\mathbf{A}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times d}
, the approximate ridge leverage scores and leverage scores are computed using the method provided in
Drineas et al. [2012]
and
Cohen et al. [2017]
.
The LESS-uniform sketching matrix is constructed as in
Dereziński et al. [2021a, Section E.1]
. Further implementation details, including those for the SRHT, are available in the public repository provided by
Dereziński et al. [2021a]
at
https://github.com/lessketching/newtonsketch
.
Additionally, the Shrinkage Leverage Score sampling probability is formulated by combining uniform sampling probability and approximate leverage score sampling probability in equal proportions.

In our experiments, the first-order methods, Gradient Descent and Stochastic Gradient Descent, are used with a fixed step size.
As done in
Dereziński et al. [2021a]
, second-order methods, specifically Sub-sampled Newton and Newton Sketch with Less-uniform Sketches, employ step sizes that are dynamically adjusted using a line search algorithm based on the Armijo condition
[
Bonnans et al., 2006
, Chapter 3]
.

For a fair comparison of the “convergence-complexity” trade-off across different optimization methods, the time reported in Figures
2
and
3
include the time for input data pre-processing, e.g., the computation of exact or approximate leverage scores, and Walsh–Hadamard transform, as well as the computational overhead associated with the sketching process.
And we fix in Figures
2
and
3
the ridge regularization parameter to
\lambda=10^{-2}
for both MNIST and CIFAR-10 data in
Section

5
.

G.2
Datasets

For MNIST data matrix, we have
n=2^{13}
and
d=2^{7}
, and for CIFAR-10 data, we have
n=2^{14}
and
d=2^{8}
.
We use
torchvision.transforms
from PyTorch to pre-process each image.
We divide the ten classes of MNIST and CIFAR-10 datasets into two groups, assigning them labels of
-1
and
+1
, respectively.

G.3
Inversion Bias for Exact versus Approximate Leverage Score Sampling under Scalar Debiasing

In this section, we present empirical experiments to compare the inversion bias of exact versus approximate leverage score sampling under scalar debiasing
m/(m-d_{\mathrm{eff}})
, as shown in
Corollary

3.4
and discussed in
Remark

3.5
, for both MNIST and CIFAR-10 data.

Figure

4
depicts the inversion bias (with and without the scalar debiasing
m/(m-d_{\mathrm{eff}})
measured in spectral norm:

\|\mathbf{H}^{1/2}({\mathbb{E}}[(\frac{m}{m-d_{\mathrm{eff}}}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{\top}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}+\lambda\mathbf{I}_{d})^{-1}]-(\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{A}+\lambda\mathbf{I}_{d})^{-1})\mathbf{H}^{1/2}\|
and
\|\mathbf{H}^{1/2}({\mathbb{E}}[(\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{\top}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}+\lambda\mathbf{I}_{d})^{-1}]-(\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{A}+\lambda\mathbf{I}_{d})^{-1})\mathbf{H}^{1/2}\|
,
\mathbf{H}=\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{A}+\lambda\mathbf{I}_{d}
) as a function of the sketch size
m
, using the following random sampling schemes:

1.

RLev
: Exact
\lambda
-ridge leverage score sampling with scalar debiasing
m/(m-d_{\mathrm{eff}})
.

2.

NO-RLev
: Standard exact
\lambda
-ridge leverage score sampling
without
scalar debiasing
m/(m-d_{\mathrm{eff}})
.

3.

ARLev
: Approximate
\lambda
-ridge leverage score sampling (identical to that used in
Section

5
) with scalar debiasing
m/(m-d_{\mathrm{eff}})
, where the approximate ridge leverage scores
\hat{\ell}_{i}^{\mathbf{C}}\approx\|\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{1}\mathbf{A}+\lambda\mathbf{I}_{d})^{-1/2}\|^{2}
are computed using sparse Johnson Lindenstrauss transform (SJLT)
[
Clarkson and Woodruff, 2017
]

\mathbf{S}_{1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m_{1}\times n}
of size
m_{1}
.

4.

NO-ARLev
: Standard approximate
\lambda
-ridge leverage score sampling without scalar debiasing
m/(m-d_{\mathrm{eff}})
.

5.

DARLev
: A more efficient double-sketches variant of approximate
\lambda
-ridge leverage score sampling, together with scalar debiasing
m/(m-d_{\mathrm{eff}})
, where the approximate ridge leverage scores
\hat{\ell}_{i}^{\mathbf{C}}\approx\|\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{A}((\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{1}\mathbf{A}+\lambda\mathbf{I}_{d})^{-1/2}\mathbf{S}^{\top}_{2})\|^{2}
are constructed using two SJLT matrices
\mathbf{S}_{1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m_{1}\times n}
and
\mathbf{S}_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m_{2}\times d}
, with
m_{2}<m_{1}
, see
[
Drineas et al., 2012
,
Cohen et al., 2017
]
.

6.

NO-DARLev
: As
DARLev
but without the scalar debiasing
m/(m-d_{\mathrm{eff}})
.

We observe from
Figure

4
that the inversion bias of all random sampling methods consistently decreases as
m
increases.
Comparing solid to dashed lines, we see that the proposed scalar debiasing effectively reduces the inversion bias by a significant margin.
We also find that
RLev
yields a lower inversion bias compared to its approximate counterparts
ARLev
and
DARLev
, under the scalar debiasing.
These results corroborate the conclusions in
Corollary

3.4
and
Remark

3.5
.
